## 9. Architecture and code review, 2026-09-12

The author's request for a comprehensive review adds a findings record to
this root before opening focused research children. The review covers the
engine, compiler, HTTP/CLI host, both client tiers and stores, React arms,
reference apps, simulator, fixtures, witnesses and release tooling at
Snapback 4 tree `cf0a13dca32b3e1721d1d947a3f82c262a0807f0`
(repository commit `d6c803878a5432cce5f045777786a049972d3d70`, version
0.0.16). It does not change the bakeoff decisions or accept new designs.

The prescribed suite passes, but additional probes reproduce
schema-admission and projection failures, lost or regressed replica state,
incorrect predictions, stale observations, compiler semantic errors and
request-worker starvation. Passing the existing suite does not establish
the missing properties. The author's updated scope reserves security holes
for another model; this record covers architecture, correctness,
performance and ergonomics and makes no security-review claim.

Four questions need design work beyond an isolated patch:

- [LLP 3000.000](./3000.000-atomic-schema-and-release-activation.research.md):
  the atomic unit and owner of schema/release activation.
- [LLP 3000.001](./3000.001-durable-replica-and-write-lifecycle.research.md):
  confirmed replica data, speculative writes and durable outcomes.
- [LLP 3000.002](./3000.002-end-to-end-resource-bounds.research.md):
  physical resource bounds across requests and replication.
- [LLP 3000.003](./3000.003-one-semantics-and-credible-verification.research.md):
  one semantic contract across Rust, TypeScript and verification hosts.

The [detailed review and 37-ticket index](./reviews/3000-architecture-code-review-20260912.md)
record evidence, alternatives and acceptance examples. All four children are
Draft Research. They remain review findings and proposals; the core owner
retains implementation custody under §1 decision 4.

The author subsequently requested implementation of the 37 concrete tickets.
All 37 are now closed; the [correction qualification record](./reviews/3000-review-corrections-20260913.md)
contains the executed checks, independent reviews and remaining research scope.
Their resolution records and the children's `Implementation follow-up`
sections describe the delivered boundaries: transactional semantic activation,
confirmed-base replay and atomic snapshot ownership, bounded admission/output,
and compiler/client parity corrections. Integration preserves the concurrently
landed native Expo module and ordered-merge performance work. The four children
remain Draft Research for their explicitly outstanding choices; the ticket
fixes do not claim that every architecture question has been settled.

The separate [security review](./reviews/3000-security-review-20260913.md)
opened twelve findings across the HTTP, identity, storage, synchronization and
asset boundaries. Its
[remediation record](./reviews/3000-security-remediation-20260913.md) closes
those findings and three overlapping comprehensive-review findings. The four
security decisions in §7 state the lasting contract; individual tickets retain
the counterexamples, acceptance evidence and limits.

## How to orient

Read this document, then LLP 2000.000 for the argument and its evidence,
then `snapback4/AGENTS.md` once it exists, then the journal. For the
sibling's position read LLP 2000; for the incumbent, LLP 1000; for the
history both inherit, LLP 0329 and its family.

## Non-claims

- This document accepts nothing in LLP 2000.000 on the author's behalf; it
  is the brief, and only running code binds.
- It changes no Snapback 2 or Snapback 3 status, scope, ceiling or gate, and
  edits neither rules file.
- It decides no promotion; the comparison and its ruling are the author's.
- It claims no result; every number above is a threshold or a plan.

## 8. The client, dev and rule seams the runs hit

Each item names the loop it removes (§2.2).

1. **A mutation's result reaches the client.** `Write` gains
   `result` on `sent` (the server already answers with it,
   `crates/snapback4/src/serve.rs`); `useMutation().run()` resolves with
   the `Write`. Ruled (2026-09-13): `result` on `sent` only — no
   `predicted` on `pending`; a prediction is visible as `pending` rows,
   not as an answer.
   Fresh replies remove Grok's DM-reuse lookup. **2026-09-14 correction
   under LLP 3000.008's delegated program:** §17 deliberately withholds
   historical values on replay. `Write<R>` therefore distinguishes fresh
   `sent` with `result: R` (including null) from `sent` with
   `replayed: true` and no result. Both clients and hooks preserve this
   distinction; a lost acknowledgement never grants historical read
   authority or justifies resubmission with a new ID. An application may
   make a newly authorized query when it receives execution identity only.
2. **A shape over a device row keeps `pending`.** `*` spreads device
   metadata through as it spreads columns; `api.ts` types a shaped
   object with `pending?: boolean` when its base is a row. Removes the
   0.2.6 M6 loop and the `historyRows` second query.
3. **`webDevice()` persists the wasm.** The opener keeps the module's
   bytes in IndexedDB by digest and opens on them with no network, so an
   offline reload does not depend on a URL. Removes the M3 loop the
   builder solved by hand and makes the cold-offline promise the
   opener's, not the app's.
4. **The `{ id }` asset argument predicts.** The device resolves the
   uploader's own upload facts onto the argument as the server does
   (`issues/20260913-snapback4-video-id-only-prediction-refuses.md`).
5. **Identity admission under `dev`.** Ruled (2026-09-13): on loopback,
   `snapback4 dev` lifts the per-peer and per-email minute counters to
   what the host can carry — the Argon2 slots and the connection caps,
   which are the physical bounds, remain — and prints the fact once;
   `serve` is unchanged. Removes two loops in each of the Astra and
   Grok lanes and the harness's backoff scaffolding.
   **2026-10-06, HR:** password seed scripts and replay adapters use the
   existing `signup`/`login` exports from `snapback4/auth`, passing the returned
   session to one `createClient` per account. Signup already creates a live
   session. No new auth verb, credential store or development bypass is needed.
   The auth card and starter READMEs teach this; the social starter emits a
   Node script for N accounts and each account's authorized profile write.
   Reruns choose login explicitly; arbitrary signup refusals never trigger an
   automatic fallback. Tests execute the published guide and installed script,
   including fresh login, authorized writes and cross-user refusal. Existing
   Argon2, admission limits, schema opt-in and dev-only doors stay unchanged.
6. **A hidden unique clash says what it is.** The refusal stays generic
   `E_RULE` where it must (§8.7), and its message adds the one sentence
   from `why E_RULE`: "or a unique key collided with a row you cannot
   read; to write into another principal's row use `upsert` under an
   `update` rule, or let a `maintain` keep the count."
7. **Write authority is the write rule (revised after the blind review,
   §15).** What Clique's counter tables hit is the binder: a unique-key
   `update` or `upsert` reads its target under the READ rule first
   (`bind/program.rs:603–641`), so a hidden target looks absent — an
   alternate-key update is `NOT_FOUND`, an upsert inserts and collides
   with generic `E_RULE` — while a by-id `update` already fetches without
   a read check and applies the update rule (`write.rs:66–100`). Two
   lanes opened their tables to get past it. The proposal, narrowed to
   what both reviews found defensible:
   (a) **target resolution is engine-private**: a unique-key or id write
   resolves its target without the read rule, then the table's `update`
   rule decides, as Postgres applies an `UPDATE` policy without a
   `SELECT` policy. **The update rule judges old and next:** an unnamed
   `update <- R` must hold for the row before the write (the pre-write
   state) and for the merged row after it, `R[old] and R[next]`, as an
   `UPDATE` policy's `USING` judges the existing row and, with no
   `WITH CHECK`, the new row; a named `update (old, next)` judges exactly
   what it names. A rule that reads only `id` and immutable columns is
   judged once, because a patch cannot change its inputs (a hidden target
   drops immutable patch fields; a readable mismatch is refused). A denial
   follows (b): `E_RULE` on a readable target, `NOT_FOUND` on an unreadable
   one. *Fixed 2026-10-08:* until then the engine judged only the next
   image, so `update <- .owner = viewer` without `immutable owner` let any
   caller take another principal's row by writing `owner: viewer`, even a
   row it could not read. The Lean oracle found it (LLP 3000.035 §5.2,
   `lean/OPEN.md` R2); the fix is `schema/update_images.rs`, applied in
   `check_rule` on every host;
   (b) **one failure word for an unreadable target**: absence, an update
   rule that refuses, a failed `if` condition, an immutable-column
   mismatch and an incomplete patch on the insert path all answer
   `NOT_FOUND` — the zero-row update — with no `current`, no named index
   and no named column; a readable target keeps today's codes. The
   disclosure granted is exactly: a row the update rule let this writer
   modify exists, and the rule's predicates held of it;
   (c) **the merged image never leaves the engine**: a valued write's
   `result` and `E_CONFLICT`'s `current` are a read-rule `get` (null when
   hidden), never the merged row; `old` in `update (old, next)` is the
   engine's, bound nowhere in the author's program; asset facts on
   unchanged columns are preserved without re-validation, and the update
   rule is checked before any asset diagnostic (`write.rs:75–156`);
   (d) **withheld before recorded**: `_ops` stores exactly the body `/m`
   returns, so a replay cannot grow a row (the existing gap is
   `issues/20260913-snapback4-ops-replay-returns-cached-content-after-revocation.md`);
   (e) **the device never predicts against a target it does not hold**:
   a fetch miss on a write is queued without a row — no predicted
   `NOT_FOUND`, no predicted insert, no `pending` row — on both stores,
   and `admit` queues that class (today `local::predict` raises
   `E_PREDICT` only past a horizon, `admit` fails on any other refusal,
   and an upsert miss takes the insert path and plants a `pending` row);
   (f) **`insert … if absent` on a hidden collision stays `E_RULE`**
   (S05), and so does a collision with any *other* hidden row on another
   index: target authority does not transfer;
   (g) **a readable maintain over a hidden source is a declared read-rule
   channel** — a writer who may read the aggregate learns the delta they
   caused; `check` notes an update rule wider than the read rule under a
   maintain the writer can read;
   (h) `delete` is out of scope.
   Not claimed: constant time; that constraint outcomes disclose nothing
   — a cap counted over physical rows, `after insert` on the insert
   branch, a budget abort near a ceiling — which the GPT review lists and
   §15 records as residuals of the narrowed contract. Regressions: §15's
   `s05_*` and `w1_*` list. Awaits the author's ruling (§14, 15).
8. **`snapback4 sql "…"`, a fifth observability verb.** Read-only SQL
   over generated per-table views (`json_extract` over the store's
   `r(t, id, data)`, `crates/snapback4-core/src/store.rs:101`), as the
   developer over the dev store or a `--release` directory, bounded by a
   row limit; never an application surface and never rule-filtered,
   because it is the developer's own store. Answers the ground-truth
   complaint of two essays and the operations dimension (53 against 76);
   the 0.2.6 Astra lane backed up its SQLite by hand to get the same
   thing. Catalog inspection (2026-10-04) adds `\dt` / `.tables` and
   `\d <table>` / `.schema <table>` under the same verb and read-only snapshot.
   They read the active generation's application schema, never SQLite metadata:
   tables and public maintained views, Quarry types/nullability, indexes and
   unique keys (including implicit id), and stored row counts exact through
   10,000, otherwise a lower bound of 10,001. Ephemeral counts are unavailable.
   Index provenance is the source-declared identity set captured before compiler
   expansion. It is transient compiler input, excluded from schema equality,
   hashes, serialized generation schemas and device projections. A separate
   `_meta` catalog record commits atomically with successful server activation,
   tied to its generation and schema hash. An unchanged adoption can refresh
   that record without creating a generation or changing stored schema bytes
   (SX round 3; LLP 3000.022's capability-free compatibility fence).
   Source-declared unnamed unique keys remain authored, with their internal
   spelling hidden. Generated indexes have no displayed name; bookkeeping
   indexes over hidden columns are omitted. Older generations without the
   record, or a record from a different activation, report unknown provenance,
   never a name-based guess. Inspection neither writes nor compiles local source.
   Private maintained targets stay hidden. Internal-name refusals print
   "`\dt` lists tables; `\d messages` describes one". No REPL is introduced.
   `check` previews newly declared/changed sync windows with at most 10,000
   row keys per table and notes a population at least ten times the window;
   totals explicitly span all groups and do not imply a per-group overflow.
   Unchanged windows stay quiet. The sync/reads openings and starter comments
   distinguish device working sets from server history and offline acquisition.
   Ruled (2026-09-13) as a fifth named verb beside `why`,
   `trace`, `logs` and `data`; the Moves line is in `NOT-DOING.md`.
9. **`snapback4 check --fix`.** Applies the spelling-class rewrites in
   place — an ambiguous key's `by`, a cursor index's missing `id`
   tie-break, an alias to its canonical form, `proc` to `fn` — and prints
   every other diagnostic as today. The diagnostic-and-rewrite loop
   exists so an agent can apply the rewrite; the tool applying the
   mechanical ones is the loop bar's cheapest win.
10. **`snapback4 init --expo`.** The `apps/chat-expo` starter with a
    Hermes-clean toolchain pin. Grok's seven failed iOS exports were an
    Expo/Babel version mismatch in the app, not Snapback; a starter
    removes the guess and the native artifact the grade wants.
11. **`api.ts` documents itself.** A doc comment per operation: the
    arguments, the result, `runs`/`predicts`, and the `require` codes
    the program can raise, which the binder knows statically. Agents
    read `api.ts` before the README.
12. **A server exit without a diagnostic.** The 0.2.6 Astra lane's scale
    instance exited twice during a schema change with a backfill and left
    nothing in its log; filed as
    `issues/20260913-snapback4-dev-exits-without-diagnostic-during-schema-change.md`.
    A defect, not a design item; listed because "sail through" includes
    not dying silently.
13. **An SDK-managed cache for ordinary media.** Asset responses are
    `private, no-store` by design, so every local-first builder — both
    Astra runs, and the Convex and Supabase builders for their own
    reasons — wrote a per-principal media cache by hand, with its own
    purge on revocation and its own view-once exclusion. Proposed: the
    local-first client keeps the bytes of ordinary `image`/`video`
    assets it has fetched, keyed by asset id and home row, in the
    device's own storage (IndexedDB on the web, the documents directory
    under the native module), with explicit retention (`assets: { keep:
    "viewed" | "none", maxBytes }`), purged when the home row leaves the
    partition, is cleared, expires or is deleted, and never for a `once`
    asset. `useAsset` reads the cache first offline. This is the
    residency draft's "ordinary media durability" gap and the received
    review's second-phase item; it makes the cold-offline promise the
    client's rather than the app's, as §8.3 does for the wasm. Ruled by
    the author on 2026-09-13: it lands with the language (§12, A).

    **2026-09-22 native compatibility:** fetched React Native Blobs may lack
    `arrayBuffer`; the shared SDK uses public FileReader and releases its
    owned response Blob after copying. A transient once handle may use an
    in-memory data URI if the host's object-URL API fails; copied strings
    must be dropped by the caller on close and cannot be forcibly revoked.
    Once bytes still never enter durable media storage. The first-party
    Swift store gives admitted MP4 a real `.mp4` filename for AVFoundation,
    migrating legacy bare-ID files within the same private directory. An
    atomically written bare file wins interrupted dual-name states; purge
    removes the possible shadow before that authoritative file. Images keep
    bare IDs, and close preserves retained files. This uses the existing
    store lock and identity validation, without another display cache or ABI.
    [LLP 3000.011.005.000 §8](./3000.011.005.000-qualify-the-built-application.rfc.md#8-actual-app-qualification-exposed-a-native-media-defect)
    records the actual-host failures, selected remedies and qualification.

## 9. Escape hatches for the last ten percent

+# Staging with real users
+
+Use `serve` for staging, shared previews and `probe:serve`. It enforces the same
+password identity and rules as production. `dev` always advertises
+`development:true` in `/auth/config`, even with closed doors; probes should
+refuse it. `guide deploy` covers release evolution; PROD2 owns starter adaptation.
+
+## Password roles and operator provisioning
+
+Save this complete source as `snapback/schema.q` in an app with snapback4 installed.
+Signup creates an account without a role. Only the local filesystem operator can
+bootstrap this roster. The insert rule enforces the boundary independently of
+the mutation. SEC owns self-promotion diagnostics and invariant proofs.
+
+```quarry
+use identity password
+table roles:
+  principal: principal
+  role: enum('admin', 'member')
+  unique principal
+  immutable principal
+  read <- .principal = viewer or viewer = 'service:operator'
+  insert <- viewer = 'service:operator'
+  update <- viewer = 'service:operator'
+  online only
+mutation provision(principal: principal, role: enum('admin', 'member')):
+  require viewer = 'service:operator' else NOT_OPERATOR
+  return upsert roles[principal] { principal, role }
+query myRole():
+  return roles[viewer]
+table notes:
+  owner: principal
+  body: text 1..200
+  read <- .owner = viewer
+  insert <- .owner = viewer
+  online only
+mutation add(body: text 1..200):
+  return insert notes { owner: viewer, body }
+query myNotes():
+  return notes where .owner = viewer first 20 by .id
+```
+
+## Stand up staging
+
+Run `npx snapback4 check` to generate the API. Save this as `staging.mjs` next to
+snapback/, then run `node staging.mjs`. Use `node staging.mjs --once` for CI setup
+and shutdown. The deployment and private credentials file remain for inspection;
+choose fresh names for each independent run. Add both to .gitignore. PORT_LO and
+PORT_HI optionally choose a port range. The printed URL belongs to `serve`.
+
+```js
+import { openSync, writeFileSync, closeSync } from "node:fs";
+import { deploy, startServe } from "snapback4/proof";
+import { api } from "./snapback/generated/api.ts";
+
+await deploy("staging", "snapback", { args: ["--staging"] });
+const host = await startServe("staging", { seedUsers: {
+  roles: { admin: 2, member: 2 }, provision: api.provision,
+} });
+try {
+  // This file contains real passwords and bearer tokens. Never commit it.
+  const fd = openSync("staging-users.json", "wx", 0o600);
+  try { writeFileSync(fd, JSON.stringify(host.sessions)); } finally { closeSync(fd); }
+  for (const accounts of Object.values(host.sessions)) {
+    for (const account of accounts) {
+      const response = await fetch(host.url + "/m/add", {
+        method: "POST", headers: { "content-type": "application/json", authorization: `Bearer ${account.token}` },
+        body: JSON.stringify({ args: { body: `Welcome ${account.role}` } }),
+        signal: AbortSignal.timeout(10000),
+      });
+      if ((await response.json()).state !== "sent") throw Error("staging data write refused");
+    }
+  }
+  console.log(JSON.stringify({ backendUrl: host.url, credentialsFile: "staging-users.json" }));
+  if (!process.argv.includes("--once")) await new Promise(resolve => {
+    process.once("SIGTERM", resolve); process.once("SIGINT", resolve);
+  });
+} finally { await host.stop(); }
+```
+
+`host.seedAccounts({roles, provision, args?})` performs additional batches; an
+optional `args(account)` maps principal/role/email to your mutation's arguments.
+Each call creates new random email/password pairs and returns role arrays of
+`{role,email,password,principal,token,expiresAt}`. Ordinary sessions last seven
+days; sign in again with `login` from snapback4/auth when needed. Setup is
+sequential and bounded (default five minutes, timeoutMs overrides it, max 100
+accounts). Only explicit identity throttling refusals retry; ambiguous writes
+do not. Partial failure can leave accounts/grants: inspect or discard this
+disposable deployment before retrying. Returned credentials never enter helper
+logs. Print them only to a private operator destination, as above.
+
+## Production refuses bulk setup
+
+`deploy --staging` can classify only a new store. Existing unmarked stores are
+production, and refuse relabelling. Classification stays with the store across
+hot deploys, rollbacks and restarts; copying production into a directory named
+staging does not change it. `deploy --check --staging` does not mark a store.
+There is no promotion flag: deploy real production into its own fresh directory
+and provision only intended users. `seedAccounts` and startServe's seedUsers
+refuse production or dev before minting a token or creating any account. A
+normal production startServe still supports operatorToken for explicit grants.
+
+### E_STAGING
+
+E_STAGING refuses production relabelling or bulk account setup on a non-staging
+host. Choose a separate fresh `deploy --staging` store; use the explicit
+first-admin recipe below for intended production users. Source edits and a
+directory named staging never change a production store's classification.
+
+## First admin on production
+
+Deploy the same schema with `npx snapback4 deploy production` (omit --staging).
+Save the following as `first-admin.mjs`. Set FIRST_ADMIN_EMAIL and
+FIRST_ADMIN_PASSWORD privately, then run `node first-admin.mjs` as the store
+owner while no other serve owns its port. This script starts and stops serve;
+restart it normally afterwards. For an already running deployment use
+`snapback4 token --service operator --days 1 --release production` locally and
+the same authenticated mutation. Keep the service token out of the frontend.
+
+```js
+import { signup, login } from "snapback4/auth";
+import { startServe } from "snapback4/proof";
+const email = process.env.FIRST_ADMIN_EMAIL, password = process.env.FIRST_ADMIN_PASSWORD;
+if (!email || !password) throw Error("Set FIRST_ADMIN_EMAIL and FIRST_ADMIN_PASSWORD privately");
+const host = await startServe("production");
+try {
+  // Set FIRST_ADMIN_EXISTING=1 only for an account you already created.
+  const account = await (process.env.FIRST_ADMIN_EXISTING === "1" ? login : signup)(host.url, email, password);
+  if (!account.ok) throw Error(account.why.message);
+  const token = await host.operatorToken();
+  const response = await fetch(host.url + "/m/provision", {
+    method: "POST", headers: { "content-type": "application/json", authorization: `Bearer ${token}` },
+    body: JSON.stringify({ args: { principal: account.session.principal, role: "admin" } }),
+    signal: AbortSignal.timeout(10000),
+  });
+  if ((await response.json()).state !== "sent") throw Error("operator grant refused");
+  console.log("First admin provisioned; sign in with the chosen email and password");
+} finally { await host.stop(); }
+```
+
+No first-user race or self-promotion mutation is needed. Subsequent grants use
+the same operator process or an explicitly authorized admin policy. Invite codes
+must be server-validated, single-use grants issued by an authorized operator;
+an arbitrary caller-supplied code or role is not authority.
+
+## Local fixture tools are explicit
+
+Password-only dev closes /seed, /dev/* and persona headers by default.
+`dev --unsafe-dev-doors` explicitly opens local fixture and clock tools and
+prints a warning; it still requires loopback Host/Origin, and personas must be
+declared with `use personas`. The local token-mint protocol keeps its existing
+private store-owner proof. Serve never opens these development tools.
+`/auth/config` reports `environment`, `development`, and `devDoors` separately.
+`startDev()` persists by default, matching native dev; pass args:["--memory"]
+for disposable storage. now() needs the explicit clock door on password-only
+dev. A proof for real users uses the serve recipe above.
diff --git a/snapback4/packages/snapback4/src/proof-accounts.ts b/snapback4/packages/snapback4/src/proof-accounts.ts
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..447c78f6b8
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/packages/snapback4/src/proof-accounts.ts
@@ -0,0 +1,63 @@
+import { randomUUID, randomBytes } from "node:crypto";
+import type { ServeHost } from "./proof.ts";
+
+export interface StagingAccount {
+  role: string; email: string; password: string; principal: string; token: string; expiresAt: number;
+}
+export type StagingSessions = Record<string, StagingAccount[]>;
+export interface SeedAccountsOptions {
+  roles: Record<string, number>;
+  provision: { readonly name: string };
+  args?: (account: StagingAccount) => Record<string, unknown>;
+  /** Whole setup deadline, including rate-limit waits. Default 5 minutes. */
+  timeoutMs?: number;
+}
+
+// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#2-real-staging-accounts — real credentials and ordinary operator mutations, never system seeding or auth bypass.
+export async function seedAccounts(host: Pick<ServeHost, "url" | "operatorToken">, options: SeedAccountsOptions): Promise<StagingSessions> {
+  const roles = Object.entries(options.roles), timeout = options.timeoutMs ?? 300_000;
+  if (!Number.isSafeInteger(timeout) || timeout < 1 || !roles.length || roles.some(([role, n]) => !/^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_-]{0,63}$/.test(role) || !Number.isSafeInteger(n) || n < 1)
+      || roles.reduce((sum, [, n]) => sum + n, 0) > 100 || !/^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_]*(?:\.[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_]*)*$/.test(options.provision?.name ?? "")) {
+    throw Error("E_STAGING_INPUT: provide 1..100 accounts across named roles, a mutation handle and a positive timeoutMs");
+  }
+  const end = performance.now() + timeout;
+  const remaining = () => {
+    const ms = Math.ceil(end - performance.now());
+    if (ms <= 0) throw Error("E_STAGING_TIMEOUT: setup incomplete; partial accounts/grants may exist; inspect or discard this staging deployment");
+    return ms;
+  };
+  const request = (path: string, init?: RequestInit) => fetch(host.url + path, { ...init, signal: AbortSignal.timeout(Math.min(remaining(), 30_000)) });
+  const config = await request("/auth/config");
+  const identity = await config.json();
+  if (!config.ok || identity.environment !== "staging" || identity.development !== false || !identity.password) {
+    throw Error("E_STAGING: account seeding requires serve on a fresh deploy --staging with password identity; production and dev refuse before creating users");
+  }
+  const operator = await host.operatorToken();
+  const sessions: StagingSessions = Object.create(null);
+  for (const [role, count] of roles) {
+    sessions[role] = [];
+    for (let i = 0; i < count; i++) {
+      const email = `staging-${role}-${randomUUID()}@example.test`, password = randomBytes(24).toString("base64url");
+      let reply;
+      // Only an explicit HTTP 429 refusal authorizes retrying signup. Network
+      // failures and malformed/ambiguous successful replies are never retried.
+      for (;;) {
+        const response = await request("/auth/signup", { method: "POST", headers: { "content-type": "application/json" }, body: JSON.stringify({ email, password }) });
+        reply = await response.json();
+        if (response.status !== 429 || reply.denied?.code !== "E_RATE_LIMIT") break;
+        await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, Math.min(1000, remaining())));
+      }
+      const session = reply.session;
+      if (typeof session?.principal !== "string" || typeof session.token !== "string" || typeof session.expiresAt !== "number") {
+        throw Error(`E_STAGING_SIGNUP: signup refused (${reply.denied?.code ?? "invalid reply"}); partial accounts/grants may exist`);
+      }
+      const account: StagingAccount = { role, email, password, principal: session.principal, token: session.token, expiresAt: session.expiresAt };
+      const args = options.args ? options.args(account) : { principal: account.principal, role };
+      const response = await request(`/m/${options.provision.name}`, { method: "POST", headers: { "content-type": "application/json", authorization: `Bearer ${operator}` }, body: JSON.stringify({ id: randomUUID(), args }) });
+      const result = await response.json();
+      if (!response.ok || result.state !== "sent") throw Error(`E_STAGING_PROVISION: ${options.provision.name} refused (${result.why?.code ?? result.denied?.code ?? "invalid reply"}); partial accounts/grants may exist`);
+      sessions[role]!.push(account);
+    }
+  }
+  return sessions;
+}

+pub(crate) fn persona_read_authority<'a>(request: &Request, shared: &'a Shared, engine: &Engine) -> Result<Option<std::sync::MutexGuard<'a, Bound>>, Refusal> {

+    shared.dev_authority(engine)?;
+    let bound = lock(&shared.bound);
+    if (!shared.unsafe_dev_doors && bound.schema.auth.password && !bound.schema.auth.guests)
+        || !bound.personas.iter().any(|name| name == persona.value.as_str().trim()) {
+        return Err(Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", "persona authority changed while this read waited; sign in with a password or use the current declared development cast").guide("staging"));
+    }
+    // Keep the cast stable through the read; adoption publishes it under this
+    // same lock. The engine's own transaction fences a replaced schema.
+    Ok(Some(bound))
+}

     if principal_with_owner(request, shared, Some(engine))?.0 == principal { Ok(()) }

+//! Deployment purpose belongs to the store, never to application source or a seed plan.
+use std::path::Path;
+use crate::store_owner::Owner;
+
+pub(super) const KEY: &str = "deployment_environment";
+fn production() -> String {
+    "E_STAGING: existing production/legacy stores cannot be relabelled; deploy --staging into a separate fresh directory".into()
+}
+
+// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#1-commission-and-boundary — classify only a fresh store under its existing exclusive owner.
+pub(super) fn classify(dir: &Path, owner: Option<&Owner>, existed: bool, checking: bool) -> Result<(), String> {
+    let path = dir.join("store.sqlite");
+    if checking && !existed { return Ok(()); }
+    if let Some(owner) = owner {
+        let store = owner.open_store(std::time::Duration::from_secs(5)).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
+        match store.meta(KEY).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?.as_deref() {
+            Some("staging") => Ok(()),
+            None if !existed && !checking => store.set_meta(KEY, "staging").map_err(|e|e.to_string()),
+            _ => Err(production()),
+        }
+    } else {
+        let store = snapback4_core::Store::open_read_only(&path).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
+        if store.meta(KEY).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?.as_deref() == Some("staging") { Ok(()) } else { Err(production()) }
+    }
+}

+//! Deployment purpose belongs to the store, never to application source or a seed plan.

+pub(super) fn classify(dir: &Path, owner: Option<&Owner>, existed: bool, checking: bool) -> Result<(), String> {

    17	export async function seedAccounts(host: Pick<ServeHost, "url" | "operatorToken">, options: SeedAccountsOptions): Promise<StagingSessions> {
    18	  const roles = Object.entries(options.roles), timeout = options.timeoutMs ?? 300_000;
    19	  if (!Number.isSafeInteger(timeout) || timeout < 1 || !roles.length || roles.some(([role, n]) => !/^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_-]{0,63}$/.test(role) || !Number.isSafeInteger(n) || n < 1)
    20	      || roles.reduce((sum, [, n]) => sum + n, 0) > 100 || !/^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_]*(?:\.[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_]*)*$/.test(options.provision?.name ?? "")) {
    21	    throw Error("E_STAGING_INPUT: provide 1..100 accounts across named roles, a mutation handle and a positive timeoutMs");
    22	  }
    23	  const end = performance.now() + timeout;
    24	  const remaining = () => {
    25	    const ms = Math.ceil(end - performance.now());
    26	    if (ms <= 0) throw Error("E_STAGING_TIMEOUT: setup incomplete; partial accounts/grants may exist; inspect or discard this staging deployment");
    27	    return ms;
    28	  };
    29	  const request = (path: string, init?: RequestInit) => fetch(host.url + path, { ...init, signal: AbortSignal.timeout(Math.min(remaining(), 30_000)) });
    30	  const config = await request("/auth/config");
    31	  const identity = await config.json();
    32	  if (!config.ok || identity.environment !== "staging" || identity.development !== false || !identity.password) {
    33	    throw Error("E_STAGING: account seeding requires serve on a fresh deploy --staging with password identity; production and dev refuse before creating users");
    34	  }
    35	  const operator = await host.operatorToken();
    36	  const sessions: StagingSessions = Object.create(null);
    37	  for (const [role, count] of roles) {
    38	    sessions[role] = [];
    39	    for (let i = 0; i < count; i++) {
    40	      const email = `staging-${role}-${randomUUID()}@example.test`, password = randomBytes(24).toString("base64url");
    41	      let reply;
    42	      // Only an explicit HTTP 429 refusal authorizes retrying signup. Network
    43	      // failures and malformed/ambiguous successful replies are never retried.
    44	      for (;;) {
    45	        const response = await request("/auth/signup", { method: "POST", headers: { "content-type": "application/json" }, body: JSON.stringify({ email, password }) });
    46	        reply = await response.json();
    47	        if (response.status !== 429 || reply.denied?.code !== "E_RATE_LIMIT") break;
    48	        await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, Math.min(1000, remaining())));
    49	      }
    50	      const session = reply.session;
    51	      if (typeof session?.principal !== "string" || typeof session.token !== "string" || typeof session.expiresAt !== "number") {
    52	        throw Error(`E_STAGING_SIGNUP: signup refused (${reply.denied?.code ?? "invalid reply"}); partial accounts/grants may exist`);
    53	      }
    54	      const account: StagingAccount = { role, email, password, principal: session.principal, token: session.token, expiresAt: session.expiresAt };
    55	      const args = options.args ? options.args(account) : { principal: account.principal, role };
    56	      const response = await request(`/m/${options.provision.name}`, { method: "POST", headers: { "content-type": "application/json", authorization: `Bearer ${operator}` }, body: JSON.stringify({ id: randomUUID(), args }) });
    57	      const result = await response.json();
    58	      if (!response.ok || result.state !== "sent") throw Error(`E_STAGING_PROVISION: ${options.provision.name} refused (${result.why?.code ?? result.denied?.code ?? "invalid reply"}); partial accounts/grants may exist`);
    59	      sessions[role]!.push(account);
    60	    }
    61	  }
    62	  return sessions;
    63	}

     1	# Staging with real users
     2	
     3	Use `serve` for staging, shared previews and `probe:serve`. It enforces the same
     4	password identity and rules as production. `dev` always advertises
     5	`development:true` in `/auth/config`, even with closed doors; probes should
     6	refuse it. `guide deploy` covers release evolution; PROD2 owns starter adaptation.
     7	
     8	## Password roles and operator provisioning
     9	
    10	Save this complete source as `snapback/schema.q` in an app with snapback4 installed.
    11	Signup creates an account without a role. Only the local filesystem operator can
    12	bootstrap this roster. The insert rule enforces the boundary independently of
    13	the mutation. SEC owns self-promotion diagnostics and invariant proofs.
    14	
    15	```quarry
    16	use identity password
    17	table roles:
    18	  principal: principal
    19	  role: enum('admin', 'member')
    20	  unique principal
    21	  immutable principal
    22	  read <- .principal = viewer or viewer = 'service:operator'
    23	  insert <- viewer = 'service:operator'
    24	  update <- viewer = 'service:operator'
    25	  online only
    26	mutation provision(principal: principal, role: enum('admin', 'member')):
    27	  require viewer = 'service:operator' else NOT_OPERATOR
    28	  return upsert roles[principal] { principal, role }
    29	query myRole():
    30	  return roles[viewer]
    31	table notes:
    32	  owner: principal
    33	  body: text 1..200
    34	  read <- .owner = viewer
    35	  insert <- .owner = viewer
    36	  online only
    37	mutation add(body: text 1..200):
    38	  return insert notes { owner: viewer, body }
    39	query myNotes():
    40	  return notes where .owner = viewer first 20 by .id
    41	```
    42	
    43	## Stand up staging
    44	
    45	Run `npx snapback4 check` to generate the API. Save this as `staging.mjs` next to
    46	snapback/, then run `node staging.mjs`. Use `node staging.mjs --once` for CI setup
    47	and shutdown. The deployment and private credentials file remain for inspection;
    48	choose fresh names for each independent run. Add both to .gitignore. PORT_LO and
    49	PORT_HI optionally choose a port range. The printed URL belongs to `serve`.
    50	
    51	```js
    52	import { openSync, writeFileSync, closeSync } from "node:fs";
    53	import { deploy, startServe } from "snapback4/proof";
    54	import { api } from "./snapback/generated/api.ts";
    55	
    56	await deploy("staging", "snapback", { args: ["--staging"] });
    57	const host = await startServe("staging", { seedUsers: {
    58	  roles: { admin: 2, member: 2 }, provision: api.provision,
    59	} });
    60	try {
    61	  // This file contains real passwords and bearer tokens. Never commit it.
    62	  const fd = openSync("staging-users.json", "wx", 0o600);
    63	  try { writeFileSync(fd, JSON.stringify(host.sessions)); } finally { closeSync(fd); }
    64	  for (const accounts of Object.values(host.sessions)) {
    65	    for (const account of accounts) {
    66	      const response = await fetch(host.url + "/m/add", {
    67	        method: "POST", headers: { "content-type": "application/json", authorization: `Bearer ${account.token}` },
    68	        body: JSON.stringify({ args: { body: `Welcome ${account.role}` } }),
    69	        signal: AbortSignal.timeout(10000),
    70	      });
    71	      if ((await response.json()).state !== "sent") throw Error("staging data write refused");
    72	    }
    73	  }
    74	  console.log(JSON.stringify({ backendUrl: host.url, credentialsFile: "staging-users.json" }));
    75	  if (!process.argv.includes("--once")) await new Promise(resolve => {
    76	    process.once("SIGTERM", resolve); process.once("SIGINT", resolve);
    77	  });
    78	} finally { await host.stop(); }
    79	```
    80	
    81	`host.seedAccounts({roles, provision, args?})` performs additional batches; an
    82	optional `args(account)` maps principal/role/email to your mutation's arguments.
    83	Each call creates new random email/password pairs and returns role arrays of
    84	`{role,email,password,principal,token,expiresAt}`. Ordinary sessions last seven
    85	days; sign in again with `login` from snapback4/auth when needed. Setup is
    86	sequential and bounded (default five minutes, timeoutMs overrides it, max 100
    87	accounts). Only explicit identity throttling refusals retry; ambiguous writes
    88	do not. Partial failure can leave accounts/grants: inspect or discard this
    89	disposable deployment before retrying. Returned credentials never enter helper
    90	logs. Print them only to a private operator destination, as above.
    91	
    92	## Production refuses bulk setup
    93	
    94	`deploy --staging` can classify only a new store. Existing unmarked stores are
    95	production, and refuse relabelling. Classification stays with the store across
    96	hot deploys, rollbacks and restarts; copying production into a directory named
    97	staging does not change it. `deploy --check --staging` does not mark a store.
    98	There is no promotion flag: deploy real production into its own fresh directory
    99	and provision only intended users. `seedAccounts` and startServe's seedUsers
   100	refuse production or dev before minting a token or creating any account. A
   101	normal production startServe still supports operatorToken for explicit grants.
   102	
   103	### E_STAGING
   104	
   105	E_STAGING refuses production relabelling or bulk account setup on a non-staging
   106	host. Choose a separate fresh `deploy --staging` store; use the explicit
   107	first-admin recipe below for intended production users. Source edits and a
   108	directory named staging never change a production store's classification.
   109	
   110	## First admin on production
   111	
   112	Deploy the same schema with `npx snapback4 deploy production` (omit --staging).
   113	Save the following as `first-admin.mjs`. Set FIRST_ADMIN_EMAIL and
   114	FIRST_ADMIN_PASSWORD privately, then run `node first-admin.mjs` as the store
   115	owner while no other serve owns its port. This script starts and stops serve;
   116	restart it normally afterwards. For an already running deployment use
   117	`snapback4 token --service operator --days 1 --release production` locally and
   118	the same authenticated mutation. Keep the service token out of the frontend.
   119	
   120	```js
   121	import { signup, login } from "snapback4/auth";
   122	import { startServe } from "snapback4/proof";
   123	const email = process.env.FIRST_ADMIN_EMAIL, password = process.env.FIRST_ADMIN_PASSWORD;
   124	if (!email || !password) throw Error("Set FIRST_ADMIN_EMAIL and FIRST_ADMIN_PASSWORD privately");
   125	const host = await startServe("production");
   126	try {
   127	  // Set FIRST_ADMIN_EXISTING=1 only for an account you already created.
   128	  const account = await (process.env.FIRST_ADMIN_EXISTING === "1" ? login : signup)(host.url, email, password);
   129	  if (!account.ok) throw Error(account.why.message);
   130	  const token = await host.operatorToken();
   131	  const response = await fetch(host.url + "/m/provision", {
   132	    method: "POST", headers: { "content-type": "application/json", authorization: `Bearer ${token}` },
   133	    body: JSON.stringify({ args: { principal: account.session.principal, role: "admin" } }),
   134	    signal: AbortSignal.timeout(10000),
   135	  });
   136	  if ((await response.json()).state !== "sent") throw Error("operator grant refused");
   137	  console.log("First admin provisioned; sign in with the chosen email and password");
   138	} finally { await host.stop(); }
   139	```
   140	
   141	No first-user race or self-promotion mutation is needed. Subsequent grants use
   142	the same operator process or an explicitly authorized admin policy. Invite codes
   143	must be server-validated, single-use grants issued by an authorized operator;
   144	an arbitrary caller-supplied code or role is not authority.
   145	
   146	## Local fixture tools are explicit
   147	
   148	Password-only dev closes /seed, /dev/* and persona headers by default.
   149	`dev --unsafe-dev-doors` explicitly opens local fixture and clock tools and
   150	prints a warning; it still requires loopback Host/Origin, and personas must be
   151	declared with `use personas`. The local token-mint protocol keeps its existing
   152	private store-owner proof. Serve never opens these development tools.
   153	`/auth/config` reports `environment`, `development`, and `devDoors` separately.
   154	`startDev()` persists by default, matching native dev; pass args:["--memory"]
   155	for disposable storage. now() needs the explicit clock door on password-only
   156	dev. A proof for real users uses the serve recipe above.

     3	/// Who is asking: a bearer session, or (in `dev`, from loopback) a persona.
     4	pub(crate) fn principal_of(request: &Request, shared: &Shared) -> Result<snapback4_core::identity::Capability, Refusal> {
     5	    principal_with_owner(request, shared, None)
     6	}
     7	
     8	fn principal_with_owner(request: &Request, shared: &Shared, owner: Option<&Engine>) -> Result<snapback4_core::identity::Capability, Refusal> {
     9	    // Declared public bearer routes keep their existing development admission.
    10	    if !(request.method() == &Method::Post && routes::declared(shared, request.url()).is_some()
    11	        && !request.headers().iter().any(|h| h.field.equiv("x-snapback-persona"))) {
    12	        dev_request(request, shared)?;
    13	    }
    14	    let mut persona = None;
    15	    let mut bearer = None;
    16	    for header in request.headers() {
    17	        let name = header.field.as_str().as_str().to_ascii_lowercase();
    18	        let value = header.value.as_str().trim();
    19	        if name == "x-snapback-persona" {
    20	            if persona.is_some()
    21	                || value.is_empty()
    22	                || !value.chars().all(|c| c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || c == '_' || c == '-')
    23	            {
    24	                return Err(Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", "send exactly one valid x-snapback-persona header").guide("personas"));
    25	            }
    26	            persona = Some(value.to_owned());
    27	        }
    28	        if name == "authorization" {
    29	            let Some(token) = crate::auth::parse_bearer(value) else {
    30	                return Err(Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", "send Authorization: Bearer <token>").guide("personas"));
    31	            };
    32	            if bearer.replace(token.to_owned()).is_some() {
    33	                return Err(Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", "send exactly one Authorization header").guide("personas"));
    34	            }
    35	        }
    36	    }
    37	    if persona.is_some() && bearer.is_some() {
    38	        return Err(Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", "send one credential: a development persona or a bearer token").guide("personas"));
    39	    }
    40	    if let Some(persona) = persona {
    41	        if let Some(engine) = owner { shared.dev_authority(engine)?; }
    42	        if shared.dev_doors() {
    43	            // `use personas` is the cast: a test expecting an outsider to be
    44	            // refused must not see it signed in. No declaration admits none.
    45	            // (Taken briefly; the engine
    46	            // guard, when held, is always acquired before this one.)
    47	            let declared = lock(&shared.bound).personas.clone();
    48	            if !declared.contains(&persona) {
    49	                return Err(Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", format!(
    50	                    "persona {persona} is not declared; `use personas {}` is this project's cast. Add {persona} to it (dev re-adopts on save) or send a declared persona", declared.join(", ")))
    51	                    .rewrite(format!("use personas {}{persona}", if declared.is_empty() { String::new() } else { format!("{}, ", declared.join(", ")) })).guide("personas"));
    52	            }
    53	            return Ok((persona_principal(&persona), "persona".into(), snapback4_core::identity::SessionScope::Full));
    54	        }
    55	        return Err(Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", "personas are a development door; a deployed server takes bearer tokens (snapback4/auth)").guide("personas"));
    56	    }
    57	    if let Some(token) = bearer {
    58	        // Reads may authenticate against the committed generation during adoption.
    59	        // Keep write authentication behind the writer: time queued for adoption
    60	        // must not preserve a session that expired or was revoked in that wait.
    61	        let path = request.url().split('?').next().unwrap_or("");
    62	        let read = detached_request(request.method(), path);
    63	        let resolve = |engine: &Engine, identity: Option<&str>, detached: bool| {
    64	            let now = shared.now();
    65	            let sample = snapback4_core::identity::ServiceSample { now, supervisor_revision: shared.worker_revision.load(Ordering::Acquire) };
    66	            let result = if detached {
    67	                snapback4_core::identity::resolve_read_only_supervised(engine.store(), &token, sample, identity)
    68	            } else {
    69	                snapback4_core::identity::resolve_supervised(engine.store(), &token, sample)
    70	            };
    71	            let result=result.map_err(|error| error.refusal()).and_then(|value|value.ok_or_else(||
    72	                snapback4_core::identity::diagnostics::refused(engine.store(), &token, now, trusted_dev_request(request, shared))));
    73	            if result.is_err() {crate::release_requests::stamp(engine.generation());}
    74	            result
    75	        };
    76	        return if let Some(engine) = owner { resolve(engine, None, false) }
    77	        else if read { match shared.reader() {
    78	            Ok((epoch, reader)) => {
    79	                let identity = lock(&shared.wake).store_id.clone();
    80	                let result = resolve(&reader, identity.as_deref(), true);
    81	                lock(&shared.readers).put(&epoch, reader.into_inner());
    82	                result
    83	            }
    84	            Err(_) if shared.memory_store() => resolve(&lock(&shared.engine), None, false),
    85	            Err(error) => Err(error.refusal()),
    86	        } } else { resolve(&lock(&shared.engine), None, false) };
    87	    }
    88	    Err(Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", "sign in: send a bearer token (snapback4/auth), or x-snapback-persona in development").guide("personas"))
    89	}
    90	
    91	// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#4-development-doors — a persona cannot cross from authentication into a replacement password-only reader.
    92	pub(crate) fn persona_read_authority<'a>(request: &Request, shared: &'a Shared, engine: &Engine) -> Result<Option<std::sync::MutexGuard<'a, Bound>>, Refusal> {
    93	    let Some(persona) = request.headers().iter().find(|header| header.field.equiv("x-snapback-persona")) else { return Ok(None) };
    94	    shared.dev_authority(engine)?;
    95	    let bound = lock(&shared.bound);
    96	    if (!shared.unsafe_dev_doors && bound.schema.auth.password && !bound.schema.auth.guests)
    97	        || !bound.personas.iter().any(|name| name == persona.value.as_str().trim()) {
    98	        return Err(Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", "persona authority changed while this read waited; sign in with a password or use the current declared development cast").guide("staging"));
    99	    }
   100	    // Keep the cast stable through the read; adoption publishes it under this
   101	    // same lock. The engine's own transaction fences a replaced schema.
   102	    Ok(Some(bound))
   103	}
   104	
   105	// @ref LLP 3000.019#3.6 — adoption's writer queue cannot retain expired bearer authority.
   106	pub(super) fn mutation_authority(request: &Request, shared: &Shared, engine: &Engine, principal: &str) -> Result<(), Refusal> {
   107	    if principal_with_owner(request, shared, Some(engine))?.0 == principal { Ok(()) }
   108	    else { Err(Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", "the request's session changed; sign in again").guide("personas")) }
   109	}

   112	pub(super) fn read_permitted(method: &Method, path: &str) -> bool {
   113	    match method {
   114	        Method::Get => matches!(path, "/changes" | "/probe" | "/schema") || path.starts_with("/assets/"),
   115	        Method::Post => matches!(path, "/sync" | "/probe" | "/auth/refresh" | "/auth/logout") || path.starts_with("/q/"),
   116	        _ => false,
   117	    }
   118	}
